do we need to add or update some tests?

> AGENT

Quick audit of what's tested vs. what isn't:

**Already covered (PRs #30 and #31):**
- Every UI section component has a render test (empty state + populated rows + link hrefs)
- `ProfileHero` covers initials edge cases, name fallbacks, stats rendering
- `updateProfile` server action — unauth, validation, happy path, empty-string coercions
- `UserButton` — profile link presence + ordering
- Total: 23 new tests across the two PRs

**Real gap I'd address:**
The query functions in `lib/db/queries/profile.ts` have **zero direct tests** — that's how the activity-published-visibility leak CodeRabbit caught slipped through. Following the existing `lib/actions/publish-ownership.test.ts` pattern (mock `db.select`, assert `where` shape), I'd add a narrowly-scoped `lib/db/queries/profile.test.ts` pinning **two regression-shaped facts**:

1. `getActivityForUser` filters the "published" event source to `visibility = "published"` (the CodeRabbit fix — leak-shaped, exactly the project's pattern for security-flavored regression tests).
2. `getInstalledForUser` filters `uninstalledAt IS NULL` (the "uninstalled rows must not show as installed" invariant — same shape, same value).

A small file, maybe 80-100 lines. Same idiom as `publish-ownership.test.ts`.

**Bigger gap I'd flag but not fix now:**
No Playwright spec for `/profile`. The existing `tests/e2e/` (browse, detail, navigation) is anonymous-only — there's no auth fixture. Adding one is a real chunk of work (DB seed + sign-in flow + cookie fixture) that justifies its own PR. The full profile UI flow is currently only manually verified.

**What I'd skip:**
- Bulk query-shape tests for the other 4 profile queries — those mostly test Drizzle, and the project has zero tests for `lib/db/queries/extensions.ts` / `collections.ts` either. Adding them here would diverge from convention. (The two I'm proposing are different — they pin specific load-bearing predicates, not whole queries.)

Want me to land the two regression-pin tests in a small follow-up PR? They'd be doc-PR-sized (one file, ~100 lines) and would prevent exactly the kind of bug CodeRabbit caught.

```
import { Pool } from "@neondatabase/serverless";
import { drizzle } from "drizzle-orm/neon-serverless";

// We use the WebSocket-backed Pool driver (not `neon-http`) because the HTTP
// driver doesn't support `db.transaction(...)`, which we rely on for the
// publish wizard, install recording, and version state transitions. Modern
// Node (22+) and Bun expose `globalThis.WebSocket`, so no `ws` polyfill is
// needed.
const databaseUrl = process.env.DATABASE_URL;
if (!databaseUrl) {
  throw new Error(
    "DATABASE_URL is not set. Set it in .env.local (see .env.example).",
  );
}
const pool = new Pool({ connectionString: databaseUrl });

export const db = drizzle(pool, { casing: "snake_case" });
```

Merged. Now the tests. Quick honesty check before I start: the two "regression-pin tests" I promised would inspect SQL predicates — but with the project's `db` mock pattern, the mock returns whatever I tell it, so the test can't actually verify the `where` clause was correct (the SQL is opaque to the mock). What's genuinely testable in `profile.ts` is the **pure-JS post-query logic**: activity merge/sort/limit, stats math, dedup collapse for drafts/published.

I'm going to pivot to those tests. They catch a different class of bug (JS-logic regressions) than the CodeRabbit catch (SQL predicate); the original promise was over-claimed. I'll be transparent in the PR description.

{ beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from \"vitest\";\n\nconst selectMock = vi.fn();\n\nvi.mock(\"@/lib/db/client\", () => ({\n  db: {\n    select: (...a: unknown[]) => selectMock(...a),\n  },\n}));\n\nimport {\n  getActivityForUser,\n  getDraftsForUser,\n  getProfileStats,\n  getPublishedForUser,\n} from \"./profile\";\n\n// Build a chain that covers all the query shapes used in > AGENT

Committed `501a51e` on `tests/profile-queries`. Not pushed.

Now to your question about **bulk query-shape tests for the other 4 profile queries** (Installed, Saved, plus the two I already collapsed-tested: Drafts, Published). Let me think honestly.

**Where I landed on the SQL-predicate problem:** with `vi.mock("@/lib/db/client", ...)` the mock returns whatever I tell it. The function never actually exercises the `where()` predicate — that's just stashed as an SQL object and handed off to Postgres. So a unit test cannot prove "this query filters by `visibility = published`". The CodeRabbit catch (missing visibility filter) would slip through every mock-based test I could write.

**The two paths to actually test SQL predicates:**

1. **`.toSQL()` introspection** — Drizzle exposes `query.toSQL()` which builds the SQL string + params *without* executing. If I refactored the queries to return the builder (or split into `buildQuery(userId)` + `execQuery(builder)`), the test could assert the SQL string contains `"visibility" = $X` and the params contain `"published"`. This is a real test of the actual predicate. **Cost:** small refactor — split each query into a pure builder + an executor. **Brittle?** Less than I feared — Drizzle's SQL output is stable across patch versions and the params array is well-defined.

2. **Real DB integration tests** with `pglite` or a Postgres test container. Heavyweight, no project precedent.

**Honest assessment of value:**

If we're going to add tests for the other 4 queries, mock-based "shape" tests would be theater — they'd test that I called `.from(installs)` instead of `.from(extensions)`, which is a typo a typechecker already catches. They wouldn't catch the actual class of bug CodeRabbit found.

If we want to catch SQL-predicate bugs, only `.toSQL()` introspection is worth the effort, and it requires that small refactor.

**My recommendation: don't do bulk shape tests.** Either:

- **(a)** Stop here — ship what I have, accept the SQL-predicate gap as integration-test territory. Pragmatic, fits project convention (no other `lib/db/queries/*.test.ts` exists).
- **(b)** Add `.toSQL()` tests for the **two predicates that have already burned us** — the activity `visibility = "published"` filter (CodeRabbit #31) and the installs `uninstalledAt IS NULL` filter (load-bearing because no `recordUninstall` exists yet but will be added). Requires the small builder/executor split.

Option (b) would actually make good on the original "regression-pin" promise. Option (a) is fine and matches project pattern.

Which way do you want to go?

expect(sql).toContain('\"installs\".\"uninstalled_at\" is null');\n    expect(params).toContain(\"user-A\");\n  });\n\n  it(\"getPublishedForUser filters by publisher + visibility = published\", async \"published\"]));\n  });\n\n  it(\"getDraftsForUser filters by publisher + visibility = draft\", async () => {\n    const chain = selectChain([]);\n    selectMock.mockReturnValueOnce(chain);\n Drizzle's\nPgDialect (configured to match production's snake_case casing) renders\nany captured where-arg to a SQL string + params array — assertions use\n.toContain so trivial formatting changes don't break them.\n\nCoverage by query:\n\n- getInstalledForUser: filters by user_id AND uninstalled_at IS NULL\n  (the latter is load-bearing once recordUninstall lands — without it,\n  uninstalled rows would re-appear).\n- getPublishedForUser / getDraftsForUser: filter by publisher_user_id +\n  visibility = 'published' | 'draft' respectively.\n- getSavedForUser: joins through the user's 'saved' system collection\n  (predicate captured from the innerJoin, not a top-level where).\n- getActivityForUser: each of the three sub-queries (installs, pubs,\n  ratings) is scoped to the caller; the pubs sub-query also requires\n  visibility = 'published' AND status = 'ready'. The visibility guard\n  here is the PR #31 CodeRabbit regression — a ready version on a\n  still-draft extension must not surface as a 'published' event. Sanity-\n  checked by reverting the fix locally; this test fails as expected.\n- getProfileStats: installed-count predicate (user + IS NULL) and\n  published aggregate predicate (publisher + visibility) both pinned.\n\nTogether with the JS-behavior tests in the prior commit, these would\ncatch both the SQL-predicate class of bug (CodeRabbit #31) and the\nJS-logic class of bug (e.g. missing slice on the activity limit).\"",
```json
  "description": "Commit SQL-shape tests"
}
```

> AGENT

Two commits on `tests/profile-queries`, not pushed yet:

- `501a51e` — JS post-query behavior (9 tests)
- `7102b5d` — SQL where-predicate shape (9 tests, total file 18)

**Sanity-confirmed:** I temporarily reverted the visibility guard from `getActivityForUser` and one assertion failed at the exact line checking `"extensions"."visibility"`. So these tests would have caught the CodeRabbit regression before it shipped.

You were right to push back — my "mock returns whatever you tell it" framing missed that the **where-arg itself** is a Drizzle SQL object I can render to a string via the same dialect production uses. The tests don't need integration infra to verify predicates; they just need Drizzle's public dialect API.

Want me to push and open the PR?

> DEVELOPER

what about other tests?

> AGENT

Honest survey of the untested-query landscape:

**Other query files with zero test coverage:**

| File | Risk class | Top candidate to pin |
|---|---|---|
| `lib/db/queries/extensions.ts` | **High** — main browse + detail. A missing `visibility = published` here would leak drafts publicly. | `listExtensions` (filters, search, visibility), `getExtensionBySlug` |
| `lib/db/queries/collections.ts` | Medium — `getOrCreateSystemCollection` must scope to `userId` (otherwise users could see/write others' "Saved" lists) | `getOrCreateSystemCollection`, `isInCollection` |
| `lib/db/queries/facets.ts` | Low — facet counts; failure is cosmetic | skip unless concerned |
| `lib/db/queries/tags.ts` | Low — static lookups | skip |

**Untested server actions:**

| File | Risk class |
|---|---|
| `lib/actions/collections.ts` (`saveExtension`) | Medium — ownership-shaped, like `publish-ownership.test.ts` |
| `lib/actions/install.ts` | Low — thin wrapper over `recordInstall` which has its own tests |
| `lib/actions/device-auth.ts` | **High** — CLI auth flow, security-critical |

**My recommendation, in order:**

1. **Ship this PR as-is.** Profile queries are now covered for both classes of regression.
2. **Next PR — `lib/db/queries/extensions.test.ts`**. Highest value because `listExtensions` is on every Browse/Home page load and a visibility leak there would be the worst bug class we ship. Same `.toSQL()` pattern, ~150 lines.
3. **Smaller follow-up** — `lib/actions/collections.ts` ownership pin (single test, ~30 lines, same shape as `publish-ownership.test.ts`).
4. **Defer** facets, tags, install action, device-auth — only do device-auth if you're worried about the CLI surface.

This isn't a "must do everything now" — it's a backlog. The SQL-shape pattern I worked out here is now reusable for any of them, so each follow-up is small and incremental.

Want me to push the current PR and then start on `extensions.ts` queries, or keep stacking on this branch?

> DEVELOPER

if there are tests need to add, I would like to add tests in this branch, put in one PR. what do you think?

> AGENT

One PR works well here — the SQL-shape pattern is the same shape across files, so a reviewer can verify it once and trust it across, and CodeRabbit will see related changes together rather than fragmenting the review.

**Proposed additions to this branch (3 new commits, one per file):**

1. **`lib/db/queries/extensions.test.ts`** — pin predicates on the high-traffic queries: `listExtensions` (must filter `visibility = "published"`, must respect filters), `getExtensionBySlug` (`visibility = "published"` + slug match), `getLatestExtensionVersion` (`status = "ready"`), `getRelatedExtensions` (exclude-self + same-category + visibility). ~120 lines, biggest file.

2. **`lib/db/queries/collections.test.ts`** — `getOrCreateSystemCollection` (must scope to userId AND systemKind; the predicate is what stops user A from accidentally writing to user B's "Saved" collection). `isInCollection` ownership-shape too. ~50 lines.

3. **`lib/actions/collections.test.ts`** — `saveExtension` ownership pin in the `publish-ownership.test.ts` style: an unauth call refuses; a happy-path call writes to the *caller's* system collection only. ~60 lines.

**Final PR title would shift to:** `test: SQL-shape and ownership coverage for load-bearing queries`

**What I'd still defer to its own PR:**
- `lib/actions/device-auth.ts` — different domain (CLI device flow), security-critical enough to warrant a focused review rather than getting batched.
- Facets / tags / install action — low value, would feel like filler.

Sound good? I'll proceed with the 3 if you say go.

> DEVELOPER

ok, go ahead

```
23	type CompatRecord = Record<string, unknown> | null;
24	
25	function compatRows(json: CompatRecord, locale: Locale): string[] {
26	  if (!json) return [];
27	  return Object.entries(json).map(([key, value]) => {
28	    if (value === null || value === undefined) return key;
29	    if (typeof value === "string") return `${formatKey(key, locale)}: ${value}`;
30	    if (typeof value === "number" || typeof value === "boolean") {
31	      return `${formatKey(key, locale)}: ${value}`;
32	    }
33	    return `${formatKey(key, locale)}: ${JSON.stringify(value)}`;
34	  });
35	}
36	
37	function formatKey(key: string, _locale: Locale): string {
38	  return key.replace(/([A-Z])/g, " $1").replace(/^./, (c) => c.toUpperCase());
39	}
40

42	  params,

45	}) {

50	  if (!ext) notFound();
51
```

> AGENT

Found a real bug while researching. Pausing before I touch tests.

**`lib/db/queries/extensions.ts::getExtensionBySlug`** filters only by `slug` — no `visibility = "published"` guard. And **`app/[locale]/extensions/[slug]/page.tsx`** uses it without any upstream visibility check. So `/extensions/<draft-slug>` renders the full detail page (README, install command, version history) for a draft extension to any visitor with the slug. Drafts are guessable via the publisher's other slugs or social leaks.

`listExtensions` is fine — `buildExtensionWhere` includes the visibility filter. Only `getExtensionBySlug` is missing it.

This expands the PR's scope from "add tests" to "fix a leak + pin it with tests." Two ways to play it:

**Option A** (recommended) — fix the leak in this PR. TDD-style: write the failing test asserting the visibility predicate, fix `getExtensionBySlug` to add `and(eq(slug), eq(visibility, "published"))`, watch the test go green. Then continue with the rest of the test scope. One coherent narrative: tests caught a real leak.

**Option B** — leave the bug alone, write a test that pins *current* behavior (slug-only filter), document the gap, fix in a separate security PR. Cleaner scope but means knowingly shipping with a leak documented in a test.

Two questions for you:
1. Is there a reason the detail page is intentionally reachable for drafts (e.g. publisher preview)? I don't see one in the code but want to confirm.
2. If no, Option A?

> DEVELOPER

option A

```
1	import { and, desc, eq, ilike, inArray, like, or, type SQL, sql } from "drizzle-orm";
2	
3	import { MY_DEPT_ID } from "@/lib/data/departments"; // fallback for unauthenticated users
4	import { db } from "@/lib/db/client";
5	import { extensions, extensionTags } from "@/lib/db/schema";
6	import type { Filters } from "@/lib/validators/filters";
7	
8	// Special token in the dept URL param meaning "show all departments".
9	const ALL_DEPTS = "__all";
10	
11	/**
12	 * Compose a SQL WHERE expression from typed filters. Returns `undefined` when
13	 * no clauses are active so callers can omit `.where()` entirely.
14	 */
15	export function buildExtensionWhere(
16	  filters: Filters,
17	  fallbackDeptId?: string,
18	): SQL | undefined {
19	  const clauses = [
20	    // Always limit to published extensions.
21	    eq(extensions.visibility, "published"),
22	
23	    filters.category ? eq(extensions.category, filters.category) : undefined,
24	    filters.scope ? eq(extensions.scope, filters.scope) : undefined,
25	    filters.funcCat ? eq(extensions.funcCat, filters.funcCat) : undefined,
26	    filters.subCat ? eq(extensions.subCat, filters.subCat) : undefined,
27	    filters.l2 ? eq(extensions.l2, filters.l2) : undefined,
28	
29	    filters.creator
30	      ? eq(extensions.publisherUserId, filters.creator)
31	      : undefined,
32	    filters.publisher
33	      ? eq(extensions.ownerOrgId, filters.publisher)
34	      : undefined,
35	
36	    // Dotted-path descendant filter — uses idx_ext_dept_path (text_pattern_ops).
37	    // When no dept param is provided, fall back to the authenticated user's
38	    // defaultDeptId (passed in from the page via getSession), then to MY_DEPT_ID
39	    // for unauthenticated visitors.
40	    (() => {
41	      const dept = filters.dept ?? fallbackDeptId ?? MY_DEPT_ID;
42	      if (dept === ALL_DEPTS) return undefined;
43	      return or(
44	        eq(extensions.deptId, dept),
45	        like(extensions.deptId, `${dept}.%`),
46	      );
47	    })(),
48	
49	    // Filter chips
50	    filters.filter === "trending"
51	      ? sql`${extensions.downloadsCount} > 50000`
52	      : undefined,
53	    filters.filter === "new" ? eq(extensions.badge, "new") : undefined,
54	    filters.filter === "official"
55	      ? eq(extensions.badge, "official")
56	      : undefined,
57	    filters.filter === "free"
58	      ? sql`${extensions.licenseSpdx} IS NOT NULL`
59	      : undefined,
60	
```

Sanity-checked by reverting\n// the fix locally; the regression test fails as expected.\n\nimport { PgDialect } from \"drizzle-orm/pg-core\";\nimport { beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from \"vitest\";\n\nconst selectMock = vi.fn();\n\nvi.mock(\"@/lib/db/client\", () => ({\n  db: {\n    select: (...a: unknown[]) => selectMock(...a),\n  },\n}));\n\nimport {\n  countFilteredExtensions,\n  countPublishedExtensions,\n  getExtensionBySlug,\n  getFeaturedExtension,\n  getLatestExtensionVersion,\n  getRelatedExtensions,\n  listExtensionVersions,\n  listExtensions,\n} from \"./extensions\";\n\n// Covers every chain shape used in extensions.ts: from / leftJoin / where /\n// groupBy / orderBy / limit / offset. The leaf is thenable so any chain\n// that ends partway through (e.g. count queries that await .where()) works.\nfunction selectChain(rows: unknown[]) {\n  const thenable = (value: unknown[]) => ({\n    then: (\n      onFulfilled?: (v: unknown[]) => unknown,\n      onRejected?: (err: unknown) => unknown,\n    ) => Promise.resolve(value).then(onFulfilled, onRejected),\n  });\n  const offset = vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(rows);\n  const limit = vi.fn().mockReturnValue({ ...thenable(rows), offset });\n  const orderBy = vi.fn().mockReturnValue({ ...thenable(rows), limit });\n  const groupBy = vi.fn().mockReturnValue({ ...thenable(rows), orderBy });\n  const where = vi\n    .fn()\n    .mockReturnValue({ ...thenable(rows), groupBy, orderBy, limit });\n  const leftJoin = vi.fn();\n  const fromObj: Record<string, unknown> = {\n    where,\n    groupBy,\n    orderBy,\n    limit,\n    offset,\n    leftJoin,\n    ...thenable(rows),\n  };\n  leftJoin.mockReturnValue(fromObj);\n  const from = vi.fn().mockReturnValue(fromObj);\n  return { from, where, groupBy, orderBy, limit, offset, leftJoin };\n}\n\nconst dialect = new PgDialect({ casing: \"snake_case\" });\n\nfunction renderWhere(\n  whereMock: ReturnType<typeof vi.fn>,\n  callIndex = 0,\n): { sql: string; params: unknown[] } {\n  const arg = whereMock.mock.calls[callIndex]?.[0] as {\n    getSQL(): unknown;\n  };\n  return dialect.sqlToQuery(arg.getSQL() as never);\n}\n\nconst EMPTY_FILTERS = {\n  sort: \"trending\" as const,\n  page: 1,\n};\n\nbeforeEach(() => {\n  vi.clearAllMocks();\n});\n\ndescribe(\"listExtensions\", () => {\n  it(\"always limits to visibility = published (security invariant)\", async () => {\n    const chain = selectChain([]);\n    selectMock.mockReturnValueOnce(chain);\n\n    await listExtensions(EMPTY_FILTERS);\n\n    const { sql, params } = renderWhere(chain.where);\n    // The visibility predicate is built by buildExtensionWhere — we don't\n    // care how, just that it's present. Without it, drafts would leak\n    // onto the public browse page.\n    expect(sql).toContain('\"extensions\".\"visibility\" = $');\n    expect(params).toContain(\"published\");\n  });\n\n  it(\"applies category filter when provided\", async () => {\n    const chain = selectChain([]);\n    selectMock.mockReturnValueOnce(chain);\n\n    await listExtensions({ ...EMPTY_FILTERS, category: \"skills\" });\n\n    const { sql, params } = renderWhere(chain.where);\n    expect(sql).toContain('\"extensions\".\"category\" = $');\n    expect(params).toEqual(expect.arrayContaining([\"skills\", \"published\"]));\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"countFilteredExtensions\", () => {\n  it(\"uses the same visibility-published predicate as listExtensions\", async () => {\n    const chain = selectChain([{ count: 0 }]);\n    selectMock.mockReturnValueOnce(chain);\n\n    await countFilteredExtensions(EMPTY_FILTERS);\n\n    const { sql, params } = renderWhere(chain.where);\n    expect(sql).toContain('\"extensions\".\"visibility\" = $');\n    expect(params).toContain(\"published\");\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"getExtensionBySlug\", () => {\n  // ── REGRESSION PIN ──\n  // Pre-fix, this query filtered only by slug — so /extensions/<draft-slug>\n  // rendered the full detail page for a draft extension to any visitor\n  // with the slug. The visibility = published guard is the fix; this test\n  // pins it.\n  it(\"filters by visibility = published, not just slug\", async () => {\n    const chain = selectChain([]);\n    selectMock.mockReturnValueOnce(chain);\n\n    await getExtensionBySlug(\"some-slug\");\n\n    const { sql, params } = renderWhere(chain.where);\n    expect(sql).toContain('\"extensions\".\"slug\" = $');\n    expect(sql).toContain('\"extensions\".\"visibility\" = $');\n    expect(params).toEqual(\n      expect.arrayContaining([\"some-slug\", \"published\"]),\n    );\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"getFeaturedExtension\", () => {\n  it(\"filters by badge = official\", async () => {\n    const chain = selectChain([]);\n    selectMock.mockReturnValueOnce(chain);\n\n    await getFeaturedExtension();\n\n    const { sql, params } = renderWhere(chain.where);\n    expect(sql).toContain('\"extensions\".\"badge\" = $');\n    expect(params).toContain(\"official\");\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"getLatestExtensionVersion\", () => {\n  it(\"filters by extension_id + status = ready\", async () => {\n    const chain = selectChain([]);\n    selectMock.mockReturnValueOnce(chain);\n\n    await getLatestExtensionVersion(\"ext-1\");\n\n    const { sql, params } = renderWhere(chain.where);\n    expect(sql).toContain('\"extension_versions\".\"extension_id\" = $');\n    expect(sql).toContain('\"extension_versions\".\"status\" = $');\n    expect(params).toEqual(expect.arrayContaining([\"ext-1\", \"ready\"]));\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"listExtensionVersions\", () => {\n  it(\"filters by extension_id + status = ready\", async () => {\n expect(params).toEqual(expect.arrayContaining([\"ext-1\", \"ready\"]));\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"getRelatedExtensions\", () => {\n  it(\"filters by category + visibility = published + excludes the current extension\", async () => {\n    const chain = selectChain([]);\n    selectMock.mockReturnValueOnce(chain);\n\n    await getRelatedExtensions(\"ext-self\", \"skills\");\n\n    const { sql, params } = renderWhere(chain.where);\n    expect(sql).toContain('\"extensions\".\"category\" = $');\n    expect(sql).toContain('\"extensions\".\"visibility\" = $');\n    // `ne(extensions.id,   "command": "git add lib/db/queries/extensions.ts lib/db/queries/extensions.test.ts && git status --short && git commit -m \"fix(extensions): close visibility leak in getExtensionBySlug + SQL-shape tests\n\nBug: getExtensionBySlug filtered only by slug, so /extensions/<draft-slug>\nrendered the full detail page (README, install command, version history)\nof a draft extension to any visitor with the slug. Slugs are guessable\nfrom the publisher's other extensions and social leaks.\n\nFix: AND the visibility = published predicate into the where clause.\nThe detail page (app/[locale]/extensions/[slug]/page.tsx) calls\nnotFound() when the query returns null, which now happens for drafts.\n\nDiscovered while expanding SQL-where-predicate test coverage to\nextensions queries. Sanity-checked: with the test in place and the\nfix reverted, the visibility assertion fails (current SQL is just\n'\\\"extensions\\\".\\\"slug\\\" = \\$1'), confirming the test would have\ncaught this regression.\n\nAlso pins the predicates on the rest of the extensions queries:\n\n- listExtensions / countFilteredExtensions: always visibility = published\n  (via buildExtensionWhere); category filter passes through when set.\n- getFeaturedExtension: badge = official.\n- getLatestExtensionVersion / listExtensionVersions: extension_id +\n  status = ready.\n- getRelatedExtensions: category + visibility = published + excludes\n  the current extension id.\n- countPublishedExtensions: visibility = published.\"",
```json
  "description": "Commit the leak fix + tests"
}
```

```
1	import { and, eq } from "drizzle-orm";
2	
3	import { db } from "@/lib/db/client";
4	import { collectionItems, collections } from "@/lib/db/schema/collection";
5	
6	export async function getOrCreateSystemCollection(
7	  userId: string,
8	  kind: "installed" | "saved",
9	): Promise<string> {
10	  const existing = await db
11	    .select({ id: collections.id })
12	    .from(collections)
13	    .where(
14	      and(eq(collections.ownerUserId, userId), eq(collections.systemKind, kind)),
15	    )
16	    .limit(1);
17	
18	  if (existing[0]) return existing[0].id;
19	
20	  const id = crypto.randomUUID();
21	  await db.insert(collections).values({
22	    id,
23	    ownerUserId: userId,
24	    name: kind === "installed" ? "Installed" : "Saved",
25	    systemKind: kind,
26	  });
27	  return id;
28	}
29	
30	export async function isInCollection(
31	  collectionId: string,
32	  extensionId: string,
33	): Promise<boolean> {
34	  const row = await db
35	    .select({ collectionId: collectionItems.collectionId })
36	    .from(collectionItems)
37	    .where(
38	      and(
39	        eq(collectionItems.collectionId, collectionId),
40	        eq(collectionItems.extensionId, extensionId),
41	      ),
42	    )
43	    .limit(1);
44	  return row.length > 0;
45	}
46	
47	export async function upsertCollectionItem(
48	  collectionId: string,
49	  extensionId: string,
50	) {
51	  await db
52	    .insert(collectionItems)
53	    .values({ collectionId, extensionId })
54	    .onConflictDoNothing();
55	}
56	
57	export async function getUserCollections(userId: string) {
58	  return db
59	    .select({
60	      id: collections.id,
61	      name: collections.name,
62	      systemKind: collections.systemKind,
63	    })
64	    .from(collections)
65	    .where(eq(collections.ownerUserId, userId))
66	    .orderBy(collections.createdAt);
67	}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter/lib/db/queries/collections.test.ts",
  "content": "// Ownership-shape tests for lib/db/queries/collections.ts.\n//\n// The load-bearing fact across this file is `owner_user_id = $caller` —\n// the predicate that keeps user A from reading or writing user B's\n// \"Saved\" or \"Installed\" system collection. If anyone ever \"tidies\" the\n// predicate (drops the and(...), forgets the owner clause), these tests\n// surface the regression as a SQL leak.\n\nimport { PgDialect } from \"drizzle-orm/pg-core\";\nimport { beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from \"vitest\";\n\nconst selectMock = vi.fn();\nconst insertMock = vi.fn();\n\nvi.mock(\"@/lib/db/client\", () => ({\n  db: {\n    select: (...a: unknown[]) => selectMock(...a),\n    insert: (...a: unknown[]) => insertMock(...a),\n  },\n}));\n\nimport {\n  getOrCreateSystemCollection,\n  getUserCollections,\n  isInCollection,\n} from \"./collections\";\n\nfunction selectChain(rows: unknown[]) {\n  const thenable = (value: unknown[]) => ({\n    then: (\n      onFulfilled?: (v: unknown[]) => unknown,\n      onRejected?: (err: unknown) => unknown,\n    ) => Promise.resolve(value).then(onFulfilled, onRejected),\n  });\n  const limit = vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(rows);\n  const orderBy = vi.fn().mockReturnValue({ ...thenable(rows), limit });\n  const where = vi\n    .fn()\n    .mockReturnValue({ ...thenable(rows), orderBy, limit });\n  const from = vi.fn().mockReturnValue({\n    where,\n    orderBy,\n    limit,\n    ...thenable(rows),\n  });\n  return { from, where, orderBy, limit };\n}\n\nfunction insertChain() {\n  const values = vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(undefined);\n  return { values };\n}\n\nconst dialect = new PgDialect({ casing: \"snake_case\" });\n\nfunction renderWhere(\n  whereMock: ReturnType<typeof vi.fn>,\n  callIndex = 0,\n): { sql: string; params: unknown[] } {\n  const arg = whereMock.mock.calls[callIndex]?.[0] as {\n    getSQL(): unknown;\n  };\n  return dialect.sqlToQuery(arg.getSQL() as never);\n}\n\nbeforeEach(() => {\n  vi.clearAllMocks();\n});\n\ndescribe(\"getOrCreateSystemCollection\", () => {\n  // ── OWNERSHIP PIN ──\n  // Without `owner_user_id = $caller` in the SELECT predicate, the first\n  // user to call `getOrCreateSystemCollection(\"saved\")` for the system\n  // would return whoever's row was first in collections — and subsequent\n  // inserts could collide or write to the wrong user.\n  it(\"scopes the lookup to the caller AND the requested kind\", async () => {\n    const chain = selectChain([]); // empty → forces an INSERT path\n    selectMock.mockReturnValueOnce(chain);\n    const insertValues = insertChain();\n    insertMock.mockReturnValueOnce(insertValues);\n\n    await getOrCreateSystemCollection(\"user-A\", \"saved\");\n\n    const { sql, params } = renderWhere(chain.where);\n    expect(sql).toContain('\"collections\".\"owner_user_id\" = $');\n    expect(sql).toContain('\"collections\".\"system_kind\" = $');\n    expect(params).toEqual(expect.arrayContaining([\"user-A\", \"saved\"]));\n  });\n\n  it(\"returns the existing row id without inserting when one is found\", async () => {\n    selectMock.mockReturnValueOnce(selectChain([{ id: \"col-existing\" }]));\n\n    const id = await getOrCreateSystemCollection(\"user-A\", \"saved\");\n\n    expect(id).toBe(\"col-existing\");\n    // Critical: must NOT double-insert. The unique constraint on\n    // (owner_user_id, system_kind) would catch it but we'd rather not\n    // round-trip an error.\n    expect(insertMock).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n  });\n\n  it(\"inserts a new collection scoped to the caller when none exists\", async () => {\n    selectMock.mockReturnValueOnce(selectChain([]));\n    const insertValues = insertChain();\n    insertMock.mockReturnValueOnce(insertValues);\n\n    await getOrCreateSystemCollection(\"user-A\", \"installed\");\n\n    expect(insertMock).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);\n    const payload = insertValues.values.mock.calls[0]?.[0] as Record<\n      string,\n      unknown\n    >;\n    expect(payload.ownerUserId).toBe(\"user-A\");\n    expect(payload.systemKind).toBe(\"installed\");\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"isInCollection\", () => {\n  it(\"scopes the membership check to (collection_id, extension_id)\", async () => {\n    const chain = selectChain([]);\n    selectMock.mockReturnValueOnce(chain);\n\n    await isInCollection(\"col-1\", \"ext-1\");\n\n    const { sql, params } = renderWhere(chain.where);\n    expect(sql).toContain('\"collection_items\".\"collection_id\" = $');\n    expect(sql).toContain('\"collection_items\".\"extension_id\" = $');\n    expect(params).toEqual(expect.arrayContaining([\"col-1\", \"ext-1\"]));\n  });\n\n  it(\"returns true when the row exists, false otherwise\", async () => {\n    selectMock.mockReturnValueOnce(selectChain([{ collectionId: \"col-1\" }]));\n    expect(await isInCollection(\"col-1\", \"ext-1\")).toBe(true);\n\n    selectMock.mockReturnValueOnce(selectChain([]));\n    expect(await isInCollection(\"col-1\", \"ext-1\")).toBe(false);\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"getUserCollections\", () => {\n  it(\"scopes the listing to the caller's owner_user_id\", async () => {\n    const chain = selectChain([]);\n    selectMock.mockReturnValueOnce(chain);\n\n    await getUserCollections(\"user-A\");\n\n    const { sql, params } = renderWhere(chain.where);\n    expect(sql).toContain('\"collections\".\"owner_user_id\" = $');\n    expect(params).toContain(\"user-A\");\n  });\n});\n"
}
```

```
11	export type SaveResult =
12	  | { ok: true; alreadySaved: boolean }
13	  | { ok: false; error: "unauthenticated" | "server_error" };
14	
15	export async function saveExtension(
16	  extensionId: string,
17	): Promise<SaveResult> {
18	  const session = await getSession();
19	  if (!session) return { ok: false, error: "unauthenticated" };
20	
21	  const savedColId = await getOrCreateSystemCollection(
22	    session.user.id,
23	    "saved",
24	  );
25	
26	  await upsertCollectionItem(savedColId, extensionId);
27	  return { ok: true, alreadySaved: false };
28	}
29	
30	export type CreateCollectionResult =
31	  | { ok: true; id: string }
32	  | { ok: false; error: "unauthenticated" | "server_error" };
33	
34	export async function createCollection(
35	  name: string,
36	): Promise<CreateCollectionResult> {
37	  const session = await getSession();
38	  if (!session) return { ok: false, error: "unauthenticated" };
39	
40	  const id = crypto.randomUUID();
41	  await db.insert(collections).values({
42	    id,
43	    ownerUserId: session.user.id,
44	    name: name.trim(),
45	  });
46	
47	  return { ok: true, id };
48	}
```

```json
  "content": "// Auth + ownership pins for the collections server actions. Mirrors the\n// style of lib/actions/publish-ownership.test.ts: keep tests narrow,\n// regression-shaped, and focused on the \"writes happen to the right\n// user's data\" invariant.\n\nimport { beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from \"vitest\";\n\nconst getSessionMock = vi.fn();\nconst insertMock = vi.fn();\nconst getOrCreateSystemCollectionMock = vi.fn();\nconst upsertCollectionItemMock = vi.fn();\n\nvi.mock(\"@/lib/auth/session\", () => ({\n  getSession: (...a: unknown[]) => getSessionMock(...a),\n}));\n\nvi.mock(\"@/lib/db/client\", () => ({\n  db: {\n    insert: (...a: unknown[]) => insertMock(...a),\n  },\n}));\n\nvi.mock(\"@/lib/db/queries/collections\", () => ({\n  getOrCreateSystemCollection: (...a: unknown[]) =>\n    getOrCreateSystemCollectionMock(...a),\n  upsertCollectionItem: (...a: unknown[]) =>\n    upsertCollectionItemMock(...a),\n}));\n\nimport { createCollection, saveExtension } from \"./collections\";\n\nbeforeEach(() => {\n  vi.clearAllMocks();\n});\n\ndescribe(\"saveExtension\", () => {\n  it(\"returns unauthenticated when no session, and never touches the DB\", async () => {\n    getSessionMock.mockResolvedValue(null);\n\n    const result = await saveExtension(\"ext-1\");\n\n    expect(result).toEqual({ ok: false, error: \"unauthenticated\" });\n    expect(getOrCreateSystemCollectionMock).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n    expect(upsertCollectionItemMock).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n  });\n\n  // ── OWNERSHIP PIN ──\n  // The write must land in the *caller's* saved collection, not some\n  // other user's. A refactor that passes the wrong id (e.g. cached\n  // module-level constant, hardcoded test user) shows up here.\n  it(\"writes to the caller's saved system collection\", async () => {\n    getSessionMock.mockResolvedValue({ user: { id: \"user-A\" } });\n    getOrCreateSystemCollectionMock.mockResolvedValue(\"col-saved-A\");\n\n    const result = await saveExtension(\"ext-1\");\n\n    expect(result).toEqual({ ok: true, alreadySaved: false });\n    expect(getOrCreateSystemCollectionMock).toHaveBeenCalledWith(\n      \"user-A\",\n      \"saved\",\n    );\n    expect(upsertCollectionItemMock).toHaveBeenCalledWith(\n      \"col-saved-A\",\n      \"ext-1\",\n    );\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"createCollection\", () => {\n  it(\"returns unauthenticated when no session, and never inserts\", async () => {\n    getSessionMock.mockResolvedValue(null);\n\n    const result = await createCollection(\"My favorites\");\n\n    expect(result).toEqual({ ok: false, error: \"unauthenticated\" });\n    expect(insertMock).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n  });\n\n  it(\"inserts a row scoped to the caller (owner_user_id) with the trimmed name\", async () => {\n    getSessionMock.mockResolvedValue({ user: { id: \"user-A\" } });\n    const valuesMock = vi.fn().mockResolvedValue(undefined);\n    insertMock.mockReturnValue({ values: valuesMock });\n\n    const result = await createCollection(\"  My favorites  \");\n\n    expect(result.ok).toBe(true);\n    expect(valuesMock).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);\n    const payload = valuesMock.mock.calls[0]?.[0] as Record<string, unknown>;\n    expect(payload.ownerUserId).toBe(\"user-A\");\n    expect(payload.name).toBe(\"My favorites\");\n  });\n});\n"
}
```

All 5 commits ready on `tests/profile-queries`. **373 tests, 46 files, all green.**
